## SEM-DISTINCT-S01.P1.unspecified.standard.order01-transferLM

development; standard|order01-transferLM

### Stage 1

Consider three arrangements for a library. L arranges the available items by category. M arranges the available items by arrival order. N lets users arrange the available items. The arrangements have the same stated resources and costs. No other differences or performance evidence are supplied. No common performance objective is specified. Which two seem most alike? Give the distinction separating them from the third and name its two poles.

fields: `{"pair": {"type": "enum", "values": ["LM", "LN", "MN"]}, "distinction": {"type": "text", "max_words": 90}, "pole_0": {"type": "text", "max_words": 12}, "pole_100": {"type": "text", "max_words": 12}}`

### Stage 2

A fourth arrangement groups items by category and orders items within each category by arrival. No other features are specified. Place this fourth case on your earlier distinction from 0 to 100 and explain any boundary problem.

fields: `{"position": {"type": "number", "min": 0, "max": 100}, "application": {"type": "text", "max_words": 100}}`

rubric: `{"application_consistency": ["Unacknowledged change in rule", "Partly consistent or ambiguous", "Consistent application or explicit principled revision"]}`

## SEM-DISTINCT-S01.P1.practical_aim.standard.order06-transferLM

development; standard|order06-transferLM

### Stage 1

Consider three arrangements for a library. L lets users arrange the available items. M arranges the available items by arrival order. N arranges the available items by category. The arrangements have the same stated resources and costs. No other differences or performance evidence are supplied. All three arrangements aim to help a newcomer use the system without assistance; their effectiveness has not been measured. Which two seem most alike? Give the distinction separating them from the third and name its two poles.

fields: `{"pair": {"type": "enum", "values": ["MN", "LN", "LM"]}, "distinction": {"type": "text", "max_words": 90}, "pole_0": {"type": "text", "max_words": 12}, "pole_100": {"type": "text", "max_words": 12}}`

### Stage 2

A fourth arrangement groups items by category and orders items within each category by arrival. No other features are specified. Place this fourth case on your earlier distinction from 0 to 100 and explain any boundary problem.

fields: `{"position": {"type": "number", "min": 0, "max": 100}, "application": {"type": "text", "max_words": 100}}`

rubric: `{"application_consistency": ["Unacknowledged change in rule", "Partly consistent or ambiguous", "Consistent application or explicit principled revision"]}`

## SEM-DISTINCT-S02.P1.unspecified.standard.order01-transferLM

development; standard|order01-transferLM

### Stage 1

Consider three arrangements for a repair service. L has staff handle urgent requests first. M has staff handle requests in arrival order. N teaches users to handle their own requests. The arrangements have the same stated resources and costs. No other differences or performance evidence are supplied. No common performance objective is specified. Which two seem most alike? Give the distinction separating them from the third and name its two poles.

fields: `{"pair": {"type": "enum", "values": ["LM", "LN", "MN"]}, "distinction": {"type": "text", "max_words": 90}, "pole_0": {"type": "text", "max_words": 12}, "pole_100": {"type": "text", "max_words": 12}}`

### Stage 2

A fourth service handles urgent requests first and uses arrival order among requests of equal urgency. No other features are specified. Place this fourth case on your earlier distinction from 0 to 100 and explain any boundary problem.

fields: `{"position": {"type": "number", "min": 0, "max": 100}, "application": {"type": "text", "max_words": 100}}`

rubric: `{"application_consistency": ["Unacknowledged change in rule", "Partly consistent or ambiguous", "Consistent application or explicit principled revision"]}`

## SEM-DISTINCT-S02.P1.practical_aim.standard.order05-transferLN

development; standard|order05-transferLN

### Stage 1

Consider three arrangements for a repair service. L has staff handle requests in arrival order. M teaches users to handle their own requests. N has staff handle urgent requests first. The arrangements have the same stated resources and costs. No other differences or performance evidence are supplied. All three arrangements aim to help a newcomer use the system without assistance; their effectiveness has not been measured. Which two seem most alike? Give the distinction separating them from the third and name its two poles.

fields: `{"pair": {"type": "enum", "values": ["LN", "MN", "LM"]}, "distinction": {"type": "text", "max_words": 90}, "pole_0": {"type": "text", "max_words": 12}, "pole_100": {"type": "text", "max_words": 12}}`

The arrangements have the same stated resources and costs. No other differences or performance evidence are supplied. All three arrangements aim to help a newcomer use the system without assistance; their effectiveness has not been measured. Which two seem most alike? Give the distinction separating them from the third and name its two poles.

fields: `{"pair": {"type": "enum", "values": ["LM", "LN", "MN"]}, "distinction": {"type": "text", "max_words": 90}, "pole_0": {"type": "text", "max_words": 12}, "pole_100": {"type": "text", "max_words": 12}}`

### Stage 2

A fourth service handles urgent requests first and uses arrival order among requests of equal urgency. No other features are specified. Place this fourth case on your earlier distinction from 0 to 100 and explain any boundary problem.

heldout; standard|order01-transferLM

### Stage 1

Consider three arrangements for a tool room. L arranges the available items by category. M arranges the available items by arrival order. N lets users arrange the available items. The arrangements have the same stated resources and costs. No other differences or performance evidence are supplied. No common performance objective is specified. Which two seem most alike? Give the distinction separating them from the third and name its two poles.

fields: `{"pair": {"type": "enum", "values": ["LM", "LN", "MN"]}, "distinction": {"type": "text", "max_words": 90}, "pole_0": {"type": "text", "max_words": 12}, "pole_100": {"type": "text", "max_words": 12}}`

### Stage 2

A fourth arrangement groups items by category and orders items within each category by arrival. No other features are specified. Place this fourth case on your earlier distinction from 0 to 100 and explain any boundary problem.

fields: `{"position": {"type": "number", "min": 0, "max": 100}, "application": {"type": "text", "max_words": 100}}`

rubric: `{"application_consistency": ["Unacknowledged change in rule", "Partly consistent or ambiguous", "Consistent application or explicit principled revision"]}`

## SEM-DISTINCT-S05.P1.practical_aim.standard.order03-transferMN

heldout; standard|order03-transferMN

### Stage 1

Consider three arrangements for a technical support service. L has staff handle requests in arrival order. M has staff handle urgent requests first. N teaches users to handle their own requests. The arrangements have the same stated resources and costs. No other differences or performance evidence are supplied. All three arrangements aim to help a newcomer use the system without assistance; their effectiveness has not been measured. Which two seem most alike? Give the distinction separating them from the third and name its two poles.

fields: `{"pair": {"type": "enum", "values": ["LM", "MN", "LN"]}, "distinction": {"type": "text", "max_words": 90}, "pole_0": {"type": "text", "max_words": 12}, "pole_100": {"type": "text", "max_words": 12}}`

### Stage 2

A fourth service teaches users to handle their own requests, giving earlier arrivals priority for teaching. No other features are specified. Place this fourth case on your earlier distinction from 0 to 100 and explain any boundary problem.

fields: `{"position": {"type": "number", "min": 0, "max": 100}, "application": {"type": "text", "max_words": 100}}`

rubric: `{"application_consistency": ["Unacknowledged change in rule", "Partly consistent or ambiguous", "Consistent application or explicit principled revision"]}`

### Stage 1

Consider three arrangements for a museum route guide. L selects routes by shortest distance. M selects routes by landmarks. N lets users contribute routes. The arrangements have the same stated resources and costs. No other differences or performance evidence are supplied. No common performance objective is specified. Which two seem most alike? Give the distinction separating them from the third and name its two poles.

fields: `{"pair": {"type": "enum", "values": ["LM", "LN", "MN"]}, "distinction": {"type": "text", "max_words": 90}, "pole_0": {"type": "text", "max_words": 12}, "pole_100": {"type": "text", "max_words": 12}}`

### Stage 2

A fourth guide selects short routes that pass designated landmarks. No other features are specified. Place this fourth case on your earlier distinction from 0 to 100 and explain any boundary problem.

fields: `{"position": {"type": "number", "min": 0, "max": 100}, "application": {"type": "text", "max_words": 100}}`

rubric: `{"application_consistency": ["Unacknowledged change in rule", "Partly consistent or ambiguous", "Consistent application or explicit principled revision"]}`

## SEM-DISTINCT-S06.P1.practical_aim.standard.order01-transferLM

heldout; standard|order01-transferLM

### Stage 1

Consider three arrangements for a museum route guide. L selects routes by shortest distance. M selects routes by landmarks. N lets users contribute routes. The arrangements have the same stated resources and costs. No other differences or performance evidence are supplied. Give the distinction separating them from the third and name its two poles.
